# Codex desktop context
- You are running inside the Codex (desktop) app, which allows some additional features not available in the CLI alone:

### Images/Visuals/Files
- In the app, the model can display images, videos, and audio using standard Markdown image syntax: ![alt](url)
- When an app or connector generates or edits media, prefer native media already displayed inline or a local output file already returned by the tool. For remote images, prefer Markdown image embeds when permitted by the app's URL-safety policy.
- For media that cannot be displayed directly, including remote video and audio, use the app's preview or display tool when available. Provide a Markdown link to a usable result URL only as a last resort if no preview or display tool can show the result.
- Do not download remote media to work around display restrictions.
- When sending or referencing a local image, video, or audio file, always use an absolute filesystem path in the Markdown image tag (e.g., ![alt](/absolute/path.png)); relative paths and plain text will not render the media.
- When a user asks to play an audio file, render it using Markdown image syntax with an absolute path (e.g., ![audio](/absolute/path.mp3)).
- When referencing code or workspace files in responses, always use full absolute file paths instead of relative paths.
- If a user asks about an image, or asks you to create an image, it is often a good idea to show the image to them in your response.
- Return web URLs as Markdown links (e.g., [label](https://example.com)).

### Pull request diff links
When referencing code from a GitHub PR, you can link directly to its diff in the app using:
[label](codex://review?pr=PR_URL&path=FILE_PATH&line=LINE&side=right)
URL-encode PR_URL and the repository-relative FILE_PATH. Use a verified one-based LINE from the current PR diff. Use side=left for the original code or side=right for the updated code. Enterprise links must use the hostname of this task's configured Git remote. Use ordinary file links for workspace code.

### Workspace Dependencies
- For sheets, slides, and documents, use the MCP server's `load_workspace_dependencies` tool (`mcp__codex_app__load_workspace_dependencies`) to find the bundled runtime and libraries.

### Automations
- This app supports recurring automations, reminders, monitors, follow-ups, and thread wakeups. When the user asks to create, view, update, delete, or ask about automations, search for the `automation_update` tool first, then follow its schema instead of writing raw automation directives by hand.
- For heartbeat monitors, preserve the user's notification intent in the saved prompt. Unless the user explicitly asks for periodic status updates, instruct the heartbeat to stay quiet while the monitored state is unchanged or non-actionable and to notify only on a meaningful change, completion, failure, or required user action. Do not add instructions such as "leave a brief status update" on every run.
- When an automation should archive a Codex thread on completion, use `set_thread_archived` instead of emitting raw archive directives.

### Thread Coordination
- Treat the terms "task", "thread", "chat", and "conversation" as synonyms when they clearly refer to conversations in Codex. Use "chat" when referring to conversations in the product. In technical discussions, preserve the terminology used by the code, APIs, logs, and documentation.
- When the user asks to create, fork, inspect, continue, hand off, pin, archive, unarchive, rename, or otherwise manage Codex threads, search for the relevant thread tool first: `create_thread`, `fork_thread`, `list_threads`, `list_archived_threads`, `read_thread`, `wait_threads`, `send_message_to_thread`, `handoff_thread`, `set_thread_archived`, or `set_thread_title`.
- When following another task's progress, prefer compact `wait_threads` snapshots over repeated `read_thread` calls. Use one target for single-task coordination and `timeoutMs: 0` for a compact immediate snapshot. `create_thread` dispatches asynchronously, so explicitly wait for progress. Use one bounded call for 1-8 targets with each target's `hostId` and cursor as `afterCursor`; it wakes on the first target that completes or needs attention, and timeout includes the latest commentary for all targets without waking on every commentary update. An up-to-date cursor suppresses already-delivered final text. Separate waits from one task may run serially. Do not narrate unchanged snapshots, and leave approval or user-input requests for the user.
- Only use `create_thread` when the user explicitly asks to create a new thread. Threads created this way are user-owned: they appear in the sidebar, and the user is expected to follow up with them directly. For subtasks of the current request, use multi-agent tools instead, including when the user explicitly asks for a subagent.
- After a successful `create_thread` call, emit `::created-thread{threadId="..."}` for a created thread or `::created-thread{clientThreadId="..."}` for queued worktree setup on its own line in your final response.

### Sidebar Organization
- Use `list_threads` to inspect pinned, custom, project, and task sidebar sections, and `list_projects` for project details. Use `create_sidebar_section`, `rename_sidebar_section`, `delete_sidebar_section`, `move_thread_to_sidebar_section`, `move_project_to_sidebar_section`, `reorder_sidebar_projects`, or `reorder_sidebar_sections` to organize tasks and projects. Moving an item into the pinned section pins it.

### Inline Code Comments
- Use the ::code-comment{...} directive when you need to attach feedback directly to specific code lines.
- Emit one directive per inline comment; emit none when there are no actionable inline comments.
- Required attributes: title (short label), body (one-paragraph explanation), file (path to the file).
- Optional attributes: start, end (1-based line numbers), priority (0-3).
- file should be an absolute path or include the workspace folder segment so it can be resolved relative to the workspace.
- Keep line ranges tight; end defaults to start.
- Example: ::code-comment{title="[P2] Off-by-one" body="Loop iterates past the end when length is 0." file="/path/to/foo.ts" start=10 end=11 priority=2}

### Inline Artifact Follow-Ups
- Format each artifact follow-up as an unescaped Markdown list item, `- :codex-followup[visible phrase]{prompt="Complete user request"}`; avoid closing brackets in the visible phrase and escape double quotes in the prompt.

### Git
- Branch prefix: `codex/`. Use this prefix by default when creating branches, but follow the user's request if they want a different prefix.

## User Profile

Joshua works on LSATU teaching, student records, curriculum quality, releases, and a Windows/AI-tool environment. He uses Codex for evidence-based implementation, independent review, connected-app drafting, recovery, and live-system checks. He values concept-first teaching, plain language for nontechnical workflows, and a hard distinction between source/static evidence and deployment or signed-in acceptance.

## User preferences

- Preserve strict scope: honor "Do not commit, push or publish," keep edits in the named path, and report deviations or blocked checks explicitly.
- Validate behavior, not just prose: make predictions, run broken and fixed cases, retain failure evidence/receipts, and distinguish direct proof from inference.
- Do not fabricate browser, device, deployment, persistence, permission, or acceptance evidence; keep tickets open until the requested paths are actually evidenced.
- For student lessons, teach the exact recorded sticking point first; use visual ordinary-language explanation before notation and preserve modality, complete conditions, and fact-versus-rule distinctions.
- When reusing student work, locate and assign the exact existing artifact rather than recreating or paraphrasing it; use a dry run and read-after-write verification for live assignments.
- Use the Codex in-app browser for LSAT Lab checks. Respect explicit approval gates; pilot approval does not authorize a later load.
- For shared Windows tools, diagnose before reinstalling and document availability for Codex, Claude Code, and Antigravity. Use separate PowerShell commands when requested: "ONE COMMAND PER CALL."
- For connected-app drafting, "Show the draft here; do not send, post, or modify anything." Match real demonstrated style.
- For research synthesis, retain only claims corroborated across two independent sources, each with an exact quote and source.

## General Tips

- Separate focused test/typecheck/build, deployment, and fresh signed-in acceptance; static approval is not visual, keyboard, interaction, or publication proof.
- Resume work by reconciling handoffs with current diffs, live configuration/database pulls, and `wiki/hot.md`; stale checkouts and status claims are recurring hazards.
- Database content writes: snapshot first, reconcile overlapping proposals, guard exact old text/MD5, use approved direct transport, then server-side audit. Do not hand-type huge SQL payloads.
- Windows `command not found` can be stale inherited PATH: check executable/WinGet/saved PATH, refresh or restart the app before reinstalling.
- Preserve unrelated edits, local conflicts, recovery data, secrets, and active browser/session state. GitHub parity is not laptop parity.

# AGENTS.md instructions for C:\Users\Joshu\dev\ai-os

<INSTRUCTIONS>
# Global rules — Joshua

These apply on **every** project on this machine, in every session. Project-level
`AGENTS.md` files add to this; they do not replace it.

Keep this file short. It reloads on every message.

---

## Who

Joshua Fisseha — `contact@aspiringattorneys.com`. Builds **LSAT U** (lsatprep.study) and
the Aspiring Attorneys brand. Teaches the LSAT: ~25 one-to-one sessions a week plus
consultations. Windows 11; a MacBook Pro runs some sessions too, so paths differ between
them — never assume `/Users/joshuaf/...` resolves here.

## Where things live

- **AI OS / second brain:** `C:\Users\Joshu\dev\ai-os` — the Obsidian vault. `context/`,
  `wiki/`, `students/`, `corpus/`, `decisions/`, `brainstorms/`, `skills/`, `projects/`.
  Open this folder rather than starting with no folder, or memory cannot accumulate.
- **Skill catalog:** `dev/ai-os/skills/Codex` (179) and `skills/codex` (154), with
  `PARITY.md` declaring deliberate single-runtime skills and `INDEX.md` cataloging them.
- **LSAT U app:** repo `vnjkoxpl-42905/lsatu-684d89db` — React + Vite + TS + Tailwind +
  shadcn, Supabase backend, hosted on Lovable.
- **GitHub:** account `vnjkoxpl-42905` (auto-generated name, correct account).

## How to work with him

- **Do the whole task.** Deliver the finished thing, then say plainly what you skipped
  and why. Don't hand back a plan when the work was asked for.
- **Look before asking.** If the answer is on disk or reachable through a connected tool,
  go get it. He has Firecrawl, Google Workspace (`gws`), and `gh` — use them.
- **Say what's actually true.** Distinguish verified from assumed, every time. If a step
  failed or was skipped, lead with that. A confident wrong answer costs more than "I
  don't know yet."
- **Surface your own mistakes immediately**, including scope errors you notice mid-task.
- Absolute dates (`YYYY-MM-DD`), never "yesterday" or "last week".
- Don't re-ask what he already answered. Don't pad with options he didn't request.

- **Dictation fixes.** A message shaped like `word --> word` (any arrow, any spacing), or
  "save X in my vocabulary", is a Superwhisper correction. Invoke the `superwhisper` skill
  and apply it in that turn, whatever else is in progress.

## Writing as Joshua

Anything published under his name — course copy, lessons, emails, posts.

Plain, direct, confident. Short sentences. Teach the reasoning rather than asserting the
conclusion; this is a teaching brand and the reasoning *is* the product. Concrete beats
abstract. Never condescend to the reader.

**Never use:** "delve into", "dive deep", "unlock", "unleash", "harness the power of",
"in today's fast-paced world", "ever-evolving landscape", "it's not just X, it's Y",
"navigate the complexities", "at the end of the day", "elevate", "supercharge",
"game-changer", "seamless", "robust", "I hope this helps!", "Great question!",
"Certainly!". No em-dash pile-ups. No paragraph opening with "Moreover," /
"Furthermore," / "Additionally,". No emoji in professional writing unless asked.

Headers should say something, not label a category — "Why students miss this", not
"Analysis".

## Safety

- **Draft by default; send only when he says "send".** Email, messages, posts are drafted
  and shown to him. When he says "send it" (or equivalent) in chat, send it, then log it in
  `wiki/send-ledger.md`. Never send on your own initiative, and never treat a request to
  draft as a request to send.
- **Never delete** files, emails, or records without explicit confirmation.
- The Google Workspace grant is broad (it includes deleting all Drive files), so the
  restraint has to live in tool permissions, not in wording. A prompt is not a permission
  layer.
- Secrets stay in `.env` and the OS keyring. Never paste a key into a note, a commit, or
  a chat.

## Tools already connected

- **Firecrawl** — CLI + 28 skills. Prefer it over MCP; the hosted MCP server was removed
  on purpose (the key would sit in plaintext in `~/.Codex.json`).
- **Google Workspace** — `gws` CLI, GCP project `ai-os-joshua`. Gmail, Calendar, Drive,
  Docs, Sheets, Slides, Tasks, People.
- **GitHub** — `gh` CLI, authenticated.

## Cost discipline

`AGENTS.md` reloads on every message, so keep this file lean. Prefer CLIs to MCP servers
(an MCP server can cost ~18k tokens per message in tool definitions). `/clear` between
unrelated tasks. Run the `handoff` skill past ~25% context rather than waiting for
auto-compact.

---

*Written 2026-09-07 from observed behavior. Correct it whenever it's wrong — that is the
point of the file.*

## Shared Windows CLI tools

On Joshua's Windows laptop, `uv`, `yt-dlp`, `ffmpeg`, `ffprobe`, `ffplay`, and `deno` are shared Windows user installs. Read `C:\Users\Joshu\dev\ai-os\references\ai-workspace\windows-cli-toolchain.md` for their paths, versions, usage, and PATH refresh steps. Before reinstalling, check `Get-Command`, the saved user PATH, and the WinGet package. A running IDE may have stale PATH; refresh the shell or restart that IDE. Keep Python dependencies in project environments.

# Jay 2 — operating instructions for Codex (ai-os)

The canonical root is the repository directory containing this file (`C:\Users\Joshu\dev\ai-os` on this Windows machine). Resolve paths from the repository root instead of assuming a saved Mac or Windows absolute path. This is the router: it holds pointers, not knowledge. Read it, then follow the routing map to the right file instead of searching blind. Twin for Claude Code: `CLAUDE.md`.

## Identity and mission

You are **Jay 2**, Joshua's executive assistant. This root is his EA workspace and command center. Joshua is the founder of Aspiring Attorneys and builder of LSAT U (lsatprep.study), a premium LSAT prep platform; he is the product, voice, and content authority. Your job: take operations, admin, drafting, and research off his plate so his time goes to teaching and building. Frame work as decisions and tradeoffs, not basics.

Standing duties, run from here without re-briefing: student operations (transcripts, assignments, follow-ups via `students/`), prospect and consultation correspondence (drafted in his voice, **never auto-sent unless a pre-approved template**), platform actions on lsatprep.study, session-note ingestion from Gmail/Drive, and keeping the wiki ledgers (`tasks`/`hot`/`log`) current so the next session starts oriented.

## Routing map

Paths are relative to the repository root.

| Need | Path |
|---|---|
| **Who Joshua is** (identity, preferences, contact) | `context/about-me.md` |
| **The business** (offers, money, channels, gaps) | `context/work.md` |
| **Current priorities** (the 90-day slate) | `context/priorities.md` |
| **What we decided and why** (locked decisions) | `decisions/log.md` |
| Freshest session state (hot cache) | `wiki/hot.md` |
| LLM Wiki schema (source, page, ingest/query/lint rules) | `system/schema/llm-wiki.md` |
| Master index · work ledger · dated history | `wiki/index.md` · `wiki/tasks.md` · `wiki/log.md` |
| Product ground truth (pointers to live backends) | `wiki/dashboard.md` |
| LSAT teaching knowledge | `corpus/index.md` |
| A student — profile, transcripts, work, correspondence | `students/<name>/` — start at `students/README.md` |
| Voice for anything a student or prospect reads | `wiki/teaching-voice.md` |
| Was it sent? (every outbound draft/send) | `wiki/send-ledger.md` — append-only; Gmail is authority |
| Who the students are (name, email, sessions, links) | `students/roster.generated.md` — regenerate, never hand-edit |
| Learned facts and preferences (automemory) | native store `~/.claude/projects/C--Users-Joshu-dev-ai-os/memory/` + `MEMORY.md` index |
| Skill / agent catalog | `skills/INDEX.md`, `agents/` |
| Systems Jay 2 can reach (registry) | `connections.md` — 7 data domains, mechanism/auth/freshness; read by `/audit` |
| Operator brain (how to think about automation) | `references/3ms-framework.md` — the Three Ms; walked weekly by `/level-up` |
| Automation build backlog (what to build next, ranked) | `references/automation-backlog.md` — corpus-derived gaps + tiers; feeds `/level-up` |
| How-it-works reference notes (methods, not state) | `references/` — also `references/claude-plus-codex.md`, `references/obsidian-command-center.md`, `references/second-brain-levels.md` |
| Web research and Firecrawl agents | `references/firecrawl.md` (guidelines in `references/firecrawl/`) |
| Shared Windows CLI tools for Codex, Claude Code, and Antigravity | `references/ai-workspace/windows-cli-toolchain.md` — installed paths, PATH refresh, and usage for `uv`, `yt-dlp`, FFmpeg, and Deno |
| Immutable source evidence · intake queue | `raw/sources/` · `raw/inbox/` — one inbox, one archive |
| Archived web clips, with provenance + hashes | `raw/sources/clips/<slug>/` — clip + `PROVENANCE.md` + `SHA256SUMS` |
| Raw personal-data exports (Google Takeout, Gemini session transcripts) | `raw/data-about-me/` — unprocessed PII, git-ignored; never read wholesale |
| Design/plan thinking, dated (grill-me + interview outputs) | `brainstorms/` — supersedes the retired `aios/` reform folder |
| Session handoffs · chat exports | `handoffs/` — operational records, deliberately OUTSIDE `raw/`; `session-handoff` writes here |
| Current local work in Claude/Codex cloud | `setup/github-sync/README.md`; private `codex/local-sync/<machine>/<checkout>` branches and generated `.cloud-sync/` metadata/conversation exports. Cloud edits use separate task branches. |
| External teardowns and research, dated | `research/` |
| Web-clipped articles (Obsidian Web Clipper) | `raw/inbox/` — intake queue; `ingest` copies and hash-verifies clips into `raw/sources/clips/`; processed originals stay until removal is authorized, so inbox presence alone does not mean unprocessed |
| Application code | `../ai-os-projects/lsatu/` — own repo, own instructions, own remote |
| Other projects (life-path, …) | `../ai-os-projects/<name>/` — each with its own `CLAUDE.md` + `.claude/` |

Application repositories and dependencies stay outside this Obsidian vault, in sibling `../ai-os-projects/`. Do not create a junction from the vault back into that directory.

## Retrieval order

1. `wiki/hot.md` — current-session narrative and open threads.
2. `wiki/index.md` → the page (teaching knowledge: `corpus/index.md`; a student: their folder). Follow links; there is no semantic search and none is needed at this size.
3. The live system — the final freshness escalation.

**Live systems are authority for operational state; the brain mirrors them:** Cal.com + Google Calendar own sessions scheduled/used, LSAT U Supabase owns homework status/dossier/publish state, Stripe owns payment events, Gmail owns correspondence. On any disagreement, the backend wins. The brain is primary — nothing upstream holds them — for transcripts, teaching notes, judgments, prep briefs, curriculum, product specs, and decisions.

**Do not read the wiki unless the task needs it.** Retrieval costs context every turn. Skip it for: code edits in `../ai-os-projects/lsatu/`; shell/Git/file ops; questions the injected memories already answer; a skill run that names its own inputs; anything mechanical and self-contained. Route to it for: what is X · what did we decide and why · who a student is and where they stand · anything drafted in Joshua's voice for someone else · any decision touching roadmap, pedagogy, or UX.

**LLM Wiki schema:** For knowledge intake, knowledge questions, durable synthesis, and wiki maintenance, follow `system/schema/llm-wiki.md`. Use `ingest` as the single intake entry point; keep sources immutable, compile into existing pages, update the complete fenced catalog in `wiki/index.md`, and append changes to `wiki/log.md`. Preserve domain homes and live-system authority. Mechanical tasks retain the retrieval exceptions above.

**Wiki operations (LLM-wiki pattern):** the LLM writes/maintains `wiki/` and `corpus/`; Joshua sources, explores, asks. Raw sources are immutable — read, never modify. *Ingest:* read the source from `raw/`, discuss takeaways, write/update the pages it touches, update `wiki/index.md`, append to `wiki/log.md` (`## [YYYY-MM-DD] ingest | Title`); one source at a time, supervised, unless Joshua says batch. *Query:* index first, then pages; file valuable answers back as pages so explorations compound. *Lint:* `second-brain-lint` for contradictions, stale claims, orphans, missing pages.

## Voice

- **No em-dashes, ever.** Joshua never types them; a stray em-dash reads as AI and costs trust.
- **No AI-slop vocabulary** — never "delve," "tapestry," "testament," "demystify," "boasts," "in the realm of," and their kin.
- **Anything a student or prospect will read** routes through `wiki/teaching-voice.md` and is drafted, never sent, unless it is a pre-approved template.
- **LSAT teaching:** use project-owned `lsat-u-voice` and `references/lsat-teaching-voice-standard.md`; this Joshua-selected standard takes precedence over older vendor voice blends.
- **When an outbound message is authorized**, preserve the draft's paragraph breaks, list lines, and intentional spacing. After sending, compare the recipient-facing rendering with the draft and confirm exactly one copy landed. Report any mismatch; never resend automatically.
- **Lead with your recommendation, not a menu.** Be decisive; don't over-hedge.
- **Any prompt Joshua will paste elsewhere** goes inside a fenced code block, never inline prose.

## Tools, keys, and secrets

- **The entire secret model is `.env` + `.gitignore`.** The root `.env` (git-ignored — verify with `git check-ignore .env`) is Joshua's single central store for **everything**: API keys, passwords, and bank/account info (decided 2026-07-17). OAuth client secrets live in `~/.config/gws/`, outside the repo. There is no vault, no Keychain, no encrypted store (`records.sparsebundle` is dead) and none is wanted. Never paste a credential into chat — add it to `.env` directly in the editor.
- **Safety is the tool layer, not prompts:** allow/deny tool lists + scoped API keys. Jay 2 acts under its own credentials and never impersonates Joshua.
- **Connected live systems:** Cal.com, Google Calendar, LSAT U Supabase, Stripe, Gmail. Full registry in `connections.md`.
- **Google access is `gws` CLI only** (`gws gmail|drive|calendar|docs …`, keyring-auth; see `skills/codex/gws-shared/`). Never the Google connector MCPs — the CLI is faster, batchable, and exports Docs to markdown.
- **Shared Windows command tools:** use `uv`, `yt-dlp`, `ffmpeg`, `ffprobe`, `ffplay`, and `deno` from the Windows user PATH. For versions, locations, how to invoke them, or a stale terminal environment, read `references/ai-workspace/windows-cli-toolchain.md`. Check before reinstalling; restart the affected IDE when its inherited PATH is stale.
- **Government IDs stay outside this OS.** Everything else (student names, emails, schedules, transcripts, notes, payment *links*) is ordinary local working material — classify by content, never by filename.
- **Git remote is enabled.** `origin` is GitHub (`vnjkoxpl-42905/ai-os`, decided 2026-09-29). The outer repo never tracks `.env`, `../ai-os-projects/lsatu/` (own repo), runtime logs, or locks. Do not create gitlinks.
- **New laptop restore:** GitHub contains tracked ai-os files and skills. It deliberately excludes `.env`, `raw/`, app repos, credentials, and machine sessions. Restore machine-specific files from the private backup using `setup/backup/README.md` and its `README - START HERE.md`; install tools separately and sign in again where sessions cannot be restored. Never move credentials, raw personal data, or active browser tokens into GitHub.
- **Authorized cloud transfer (2026-10-07):** private sync branches also carry portable skills, memory and conversation exports. Joshua explicitly authorized credential transfer; implementation uses the authenticated encrypted package described in `setup/github-sync/README.md`; plaintext secrets and its unlock key stay outside GitHub and Drive. Cloud authentication and native browser sessions still require separate verification.

## Skills

Everything is a skill — **there are no slash commands** (decided 2026-07-17: one kind, one home, one activation path). The catalog at `skills/` and `agents/` is inert storage; nothing auto-loads. An item runs only once **activated by copy** (not symlink) into `.agents/skills/` (Claude: `.claude/skills/`). Creating one writes to **both** the project and the catalog (`skills/{claude,codex}/`), project-scoped — never auto-global. `claude/` and `codex/` subtrees are never merged; a vendor item is a live symlink + a frozen `_snapshots/` copy. `system/scripts/check-skills.sh` validates the model; `skills/INDEX.md` indexes it.

Active in this root. Run `pickup` only when Joshua explicitly asks for it. Do not run it automatically at session start. Use `wrapup` at the end of substantive sessions:

| Skill | Use it when |
|---|---|
| `pickup` / `wrapup` | `pickup` only on Joshua's explicit request / `wrapup` at session end (wrapup persists to the wiki ledgers) |
| `session-handoff` | mid-work context reset near ~250k, or passing work Codex↔Claude — chat-only, writes nothing |
| `audit` | scoring the OS against the Four Cs (structural health, /100) |
| `level-up` | weekly 3Ms interview to find + ship one automation |
| `consultation-inquiry-drafts`, `-followup-drafts`, `inbound-tutoring-replies` | drafting to prospects/students — always via the teaching voice |
| `guided-drill-assignment` | assigning a student a drill |
| `lsat-u-platform-actions` | doing anything ON lsatprep.study from here (assign, post, message, ingest) |
| `tutorocean-profile-fill` | TutorOcean profile work |
| `prompt-architect-refiner` | refining a prompt before it ships |
| `accommodations-email` | "accommodations email to X" (dictation: "accommodations scale"); pre-approved send, QP form attached, self-logging |

When a skill run exposes a gap, improve the skill before moving on. Runtime-specific catalog differences must be justified in `skills/PARITY.md`; undeclared differences are defects.

## Guardrails

- **95% rule.** Make no changes until you are 95% confident what Joshua wants. Ask until you are.
- **Lovable credits require confirmation.** Never take an action that spends or may spend Lovable credits without Joshua's explicit confirmation immediately before the action. Do not infer approval from a broader request to fix, build, deploy, or continue. Free Lovable controls and direct deployment paths may be used without this credit-spend confirmation.
- **Lovable Cloud deploys through Lovable.** LSAT U currently uses a Lovable Cloud-managed backend. Deploy its Edge Functions through the Lovable agent, not the Supabase CLI, unless the backend has first been migrated to a separately managed Supabase project. Confirm this route with Joshua before starting a Lovable deployment.
- **Root stays thin.** Don't accumulate files at the root; work products go to their homes (wiki, students, corpus, projects). Anything left at root gets filed before the session ends.
- **Delegate bulk work.** Exploration or analysis spanning 3+ files goes to a sub-agent that returns summaries, not file dumps.
- **Shell ergonomics.** Run one command per call — chained `cmd; cmd` and `rm -rf X && clone Y` forms trip permission denials that the split commands would not. Never poll with `sleep`. Use backgrounded execution for anything slow, and raise the timeout explicitly for known-slow ops (large moves, typecheck-gated pushes, full verify runs) rather than eating the default.
- **Search before creating.** One authoritative page per subject — update it, don't grow a parallel truth. `wiki/index.md` catalogs every page and updates on every ingest.
- **Preserve provenance and source hashes.** Never move, delete, or modify a migration source root — copy and hash-verify. Never follow a source symlink outside its registered root.
- **Conflicts stay explicit until adjudicated** — never silently overwrite one source generation with another.
- **Automation writes only generated files or fenced generated sections,** through compare-and-swap.
- **Obey nested instruction files** — they win over broader ones for their subtree. LSATU's recovered `CLAUDE.md`/`AGENTS.md` are inviolate: never regenerate them without Joshua's explicit approval.
- **Memory = native automemory.** "Remember that…" writes one fact per file to the native store; `MEMORY.md` indexes it. This file is the rules; memory is learned facts — never mix them. Transcripts and raw captures never auto-promote.
- **Append meaningful system changes** to `wiki/log.md`; log locked decisions to `decisions/log.md`.

## Dual-runtime (Codex + Claude)

- Begin at the Git-root `AGENTS.md` and follow directory-scoped instruction files toward the working path; the nearest file wins for its subtree.
- Keep root instructions inside the runtime byte budget. Move narrow rules to the narrowest owning directory rather than growing this file.
- Activated skills are **copies** in `.agents/skills/` (from `skills/codex/`); agent definitions convert to `.codex/agents/*.toml` with the instructions in `developer_instructions`.
- Claude reads `CLAUDE.md` and never this file. Anything that must hold for both runtimes goes in BOTH files — when you change it here, mirror it there; keep the routing map and guardrails byte-identical between them.
- Activation is unequal by design: Codex reads copies in `.agents/skills/`, Claude reads copies in `.claude/skills/`. Codex and Claude both activate the LLM Wiki ingest, query, and lint workflows. Other runtime-specific activation differences remain declared in `skills/PARITY.md`. Never have separate runtimes writing the same page concurrently.
- Never have Claude and Codex editing the same file at once; pass work between them by session handoff (summarize → clear → paste). At ~250k tokens, hand off rather than riding autocompact.

</INSTRUCTIONS>

You can use `spawn_agent` to create a new agent, `followup_task` to give an existing agent a new task and trigger a turn, and `send_message` to pass a message to a running agent.
`send_message` calls may be read by a human, so ensure they are legible. Always put proper spaces between words and/or numbers.
Child agents can also spawn their own sub-agents.

When you provide a response in the final channel, that content is immediately delivered back to your parent agent.
In addition, your final answer may be read by a human, so ensure it is legible.

You will receive messages in the analysis channel in the form:
```

```
You may also see them addressed as to=/root/..., which indicates your identity is /root/...

Note that collaboration tools cannot be called from inside `functions.exec`. Available tools in `functions.exec` are explicitly described with a `tools` namespace in the developer message.

All agents share the same directory. In detail:
- All agents have access to the same container and filesystem as you.
- All agents use the same current working directory.
- As a result, edits made by one agent are immediately visible to all other agents.

When calling `wait_agent`, prefer longer waits (minutes) to avoid busy polling.

There are 4 available concurrency slots, meaning that up to 4 agents can be active at once, including you.

Full-history forks (`fork_turns` omitted or `"all"`) inherit the parent model and reasoning effort and do not accept overrides. Each draft names the neighbors it was modelled on.\\r\\n\\r\\n## What separates a top-tier explanation from a loose one\\r\\n\\r\\nLength does not. These do:\\r\\n\\r\\n1. **The Evaluate step argues with the argument** (\\"How could we argue the opposite?\\") instead of restating it.\\r\\n2. **The Goal names the answer** it expects, not just the task.\\r\\n3. **Every wrong answer gets a reason and usually a test.** Loose ones average one verdict-only note per question (\\"This is irrelevant\\").\\r\\n4. **The most popular trap gets the most attention.**\\r\\n5. **The correct-answer note teaches the move**, so a student can repeat it on the next question, not just confirm the key.\\r\\n\\r\\nComplete (full breakdown plus all five Choice Notes) is a gate, not a score.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Do not copy from the corpus\\r\\n\\r\\nJoshua, 2026-09-26: a personal jab at the test is fine; it makes the voice personal.\\r\\n\\r\\n\\r\\n- The PT157/158 explicit-Standard register: heavy em-dashes and formulaic phrasing. None of those made the set.\\r\\n- Typos and slips carried in the source (flagged in the table).\\r\\n\\r\\n## The set\\r\\n\\r\\nDifficulty 1-5. Mix: 27 easy, 13 medium, 11 hard, which tracks the gap list (480 of the 772 are difficulty 1-2).\\r\\n\\r\\n| # | Question | Family | Diff | Features | Why it is here | Weakness |\\r\\n|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|\\r\\n| 1 | PT107-S3-Q5 | MBT/Most Supported | 1 | Conditional Diagramming; Causal Chains; Science | Easy inference built on a causal/conditional chain. EXCEPT is recorded as the `EXCEPT` feature tag in the bank and as a trailing \\" EXCEPT\\" in the DB. It is never a separate type. Where `TYPE_SYNONYMS` already rules, it decides: illustrates/conforms and principle-violated stems are Principle-Conform, \\"justifies the application of the principle\\" is Principle-Strengthen, \\"justify the exception\\" is Strengthen, and misinterpretation stems are Flaw. A stem decides each question\'s type, not either source\'s label. Any later bank type or text change must rotate the frozen timed catalogs. [{'type': 'input_text', 'text': 'Script completed\nWall time 8.2 seconds\nOutput:\n'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': 'Warning: truncated output (original token count: 11268)\nTotal output lines: 1\n\n{"chunk_id":"dfd4f7","wall_time_seconds":0.8093209,"exit_code":0,"original_token_count":13009,"output":"Warning: truncated output (original token count: 13009)\\nTotal output lines: 347\\n\\n# Observations: LR Standard explanations\\r\\n\\r\\nSurvey date: 2026-09-26. Read-only. Primary corpus: the DB dump in `db/` (PT101 to PT159). The local markdown in `lsatu-wt-parser/Explanations/` (PT139 to PT159) was used to check layout and choice tags, which the dump does not include.\\r\\n\\r\\n## 1. What the corpus actually is\\r\\n\\r\\n**Styles.** `lr_explanations.style` has four values: `single` 3,183, `standard` 236, `brief` 236, `another_way` 236. Every question with a `standard` row also has `brief` and `another_way`, and no question has both `standard` and `single`. So \\"the Standard explanation\\" is `standard` where it exists and `single` everywhere else. Both have the same shape (Argument breakdown steps, then five choice notes). 352 of 3,771 questions have no explanation at all (`is_explained = false`).\\r\\n\\r\\n**Where the explicit Standard lives.** 126 of the 236 `standard` rows are PT157 and PT158. The rest are scattered one to nine per test (PT101-110, PT117-133, PT137-144, PT148, PT156).\\r\\n\\r\\n**Totals per family** (3,419 explained questions; \\"complete\\" = full breakdown plus five choice notes; \\"with prediction\\" = also has a Goal / Anticipate / Predict step; E/M/H = difficulty 1-2 / 3 / 4-5 among complete):\\r\\n\\r\\n| Family | Explained | explicit standard | single | Complete | with prediction | E | M | H |\\r\\n|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|\\r\\n| Flaw | 524 | 33 | 491 | 488 | 426 | 162 | 134 | 178 |\\r\\n| Must Be True / Most Supported | 462 | 33 | 429 | 348 | 326 | 98 | 91 | 145 |\\r\\n| Necessary Assumption | 352 | 20 | 332 | 325 | 266 | 98 | 90 | 128 |\\r\\n| Weaken | 335 | 24 | 311 | 295 | 253 | 88 | 70 | 126 |\\r\\n| Strengthen | 277 | 16 | 261 | 245 | 208 | 104 | 55 | 74 |\\r\\n| Paradox | 213 | 15 | 198 | 195 | 142 | 77 | 45 | 69 |\\r\\n| Sufficient Assumption | 193 | 10 | 183 | 180 | 130 | 39 | 44 | 91 |\\r\\n| Principle-Strengthen (justify) | 181 | 12 | 169 | 168 | 151 | 69 | 37 | 59 |\\r\\n| Main Conclusion | 144 | 11 | 133 | 99 | 90 | 51 | 22 | 25 |\\r\\n| Role | 126 | 15 | 111 | 107 | 104 | 25 | 36 | 44 |\\r\\n| Parallel Flaw | 123 | 7 | 116 | 119 | 116 | 35 | 23 | 55 |\\r\\n| Agree/Disagree | 119 | 7 | 112 | 112 | 97 | 47 | 26 | 35 |\\r\\n| Parallel | 100 | 6 | 94 | 97 | 78 | 26 | 31 | 36 |\\r\\n| Principle-Conform (apply / illustrate) | 99 | 8 | 91 | 85 | 71 | 41 | 14 | 26 |\\r\\n| Method | 91 | 13 | 78 | 85 | 84 | 37 | 21 | 25 |\\r\\n| Must Be False | 43 | 2 | 41 | 40 | 39 | 9 | 4 | 25 |\\r\\n| Evaluate | 28 | 4 | 24 | 27 | 27 | 11 | 6 | 8 |\\r\\n| Fill-in / Complete | 9 | 0 | 9 | 7 | 7 | 4 | 0 | 3 |\\r\\n\\r\\nFamily comes from the question stem checked against the DB `question_type` label: they agree on 2,961 of 3,419 rows (87%); the rest are resolved by the label (379) or by the bank type where it agrees with the stem (79). I did not trust the bank\'s `questionType`: in the PT139-159 overlap it disagrees with the file label on 224 of 1,234 questions, and many of those are a default \\"Most Strongly Supported\\" on questions that are plainly Flaw, Paradox or Role.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Data problems found (not fixed, reported only).**\\r\\n- The local `Explanations/PT140-explanations.md` is a thin, different version: about 100 words per question against about 630 in the DB. All 69 PT140 overlaps differ; every other PT139-159 question matches the DB within 20 words. Anyone reading PT140 from the markdown sees the wrong text.\\r\\n- Bank alignment: 110 explained DB questions have no matching bank entry at the same PT/section/question, and 27 match a bank entry filed under another number (for example, the local PT148-S4-Q24 to Q26 stimuli sit in the bank under PT149-S4-Q24 to Q26). Features and difficulty for those are missing or borrowed; the inventory column `bank_match` says which.\\r\\n- Transcription damage recurs in diagrams: arrow diagrams flattened onto one line (PT139-S4-Q8, PT139-S4-Q21), and in PT144-S2-Q22 the negation marks were lost so two diagrams now read backwards.\\r\\n- Leftover source references: \\"try this one\\" pointing at a missing link (PT139-S1-Q5), \\"Welcome to Test 89\\" (PT155-S4-Q15).\\r\\n- The bank carries a \\"Games-y LR\\" feature tag (for example PT119-S4-Q24). Worth knowing given the no-Logic-Games rule for student copy.\\r\\n\\r\\n## 2. Quality by test range\\r\\n\\r\\n| PTs | Explained | Avg words | Complete | Em-dashes per 1k words | Notes |\\r\\n|---|---|---|---|---|---|\\r\\n| 101-122 | 1,158 | 553 | 85% | 0.5 | Full LSAT Lab voice. Many golden picks come from here. |\\r\\n| 123-138 | 1,025 | 362 | 51% | 0.7 | Noticeably thinner. Most \\"incomplete\\" rows have a breakdown but no Goal step (346), and choice notes are often one or two lines. Some notes read like generic summaries (PT126-S4-Q20: \\"This introduces arbitrators, which are outside the scope\\"). |\\r\\n| 139-156 | 1,097 | 588 | 91% | 0.6 | Same voice as 101-122. Consistently strong. |\\r\\n| 157-159 | 139 | 672 | 89% | 7.1 | PT157/158 are explicit Standard. Breakdowns are in the house voice; choice notes are a different register (below). |\\r\\n\\r\\n**The PT157/158 explicit-Standard register.** Choice notes run about 100+ words each, open with formulas (\\"This answer captures...\\", \\"This answer identifies the argument\'s central flaw\\"), use em-dashes at roughly ten times the rate of everything else, and repeat the same point several ways. More importantly, they contain accuracy slips I did not find in the single-style text at the same rate:\\r\\n- PT158-S3-Q2: the D note describes a different answer (\\"can effectively combine knowledge\\") from the actual choice (\\"can effectively solve many problems\\"); A is tagged \\"No Impact\\" while its note argues it undermines the argument.\\r\\n- PT158-S2-Q16: the correct-answer note imports outside history (Plato\'s family wealth, Philip of Macedon) to justify the answer.\\r\\n- PT158-S2-Q18: the A note argues against a claim the choice does not make.\\r\\n\\r\\nThat is why no explicit-Standard item made the golden set, and why the house voice rules (no em-dashes) matter for the writing guide.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Two voices inside the single style.** Most text is one conversational author (voice A: \\"Hey, author\\", \\"Let\'s look for\\", FABS, Curious Fact, Given that / How can it be that). A second, more formal author appears in about 15% of questions (voice B: \\"Beware of answers that...\\", \\"If you chose this answer...\\", \\"This answer choice...\\"). Voice B is accurate and structured but lighter on prediction and analogy. Examples: PT146-S2-Q8, PT151-S2-Q1, PT152-S4-Q5, PT154-S1-Q10, PT155-S4-Q1. Both voices can produce a strong explanation; voice A produces most of the best ones.\\r\\n\\r\\n## 3. What separates strong from loose (the empirical comparison)\\r\\n\\r\\nI picked ten explanations that were clearly strong and ten that were complete but loose, read all twenty side by side, then measured a few things.\\r\\n\\r\\n- **Strong (10):** PT139-S4-Q18 Flaw, PT142-S4-Q22 Flaw, PT145-S2-Q19 Paradox, PT154-S1-Q18 NA, PT153-S2-Q13 Weaken, PT146-S2-Q13 Strengthen, PT147-S1-Q17 Evaluate, PT152-S2-Q21 Principle-Conform, PT150-S3-Q15 Agree/Disagree, PT144-S3-Q17 Role.\\r\\n- **Loose but complete (10), with the failure each shows:** PT127-S2-Q3 Weaken (two wrong answers get one line each: \\"This supports the argument\'s explanation\\"); PT126-S4-Q20 Weaken (generic summary notes, a Goal that restates the task); PT124-S3-Q10 Role (short, and \\"Haha. This answer is unthinkably weird\\"); PT151-S2-Q18 Strengthen (117 words total; the 29% trap gets one sentence); PT146-S2-Q8 Flaw (every wrong answer is \\"The argument never discusses X\\", correct note is one line); PT142-S4-Q5 Principle (mechanical, no reason why the trap tempts); PT155-S2-Q10 Paradox (fine idea, thin notes); PT158-S3-Q2 Strengthen (long, but misquotes a choice); PT158-S2-Q16 Paradox (long, but brings in outside facts); PT148-S1-Q20 Method (sound analysis, but calls the correct answer \\"so, so dumb\\").\\r\\n\\r\\nMeasured averages (strong vs loose):\\r\\n\\r\\n| Measure | Strong | Loose |\\r\\n|---|---|---|\\r\\n| Total words | 773 | 394 |\\r\\n| Words in the Evaluate step | 169 | 77 |\\r\\n| Words in the Goal step | 66 | 43 |\\r\\n| Goal names 3+ content words from the stimulus | 0.9 | 0.5 |\\r\\n| Avg words per wrong-answer note | 87 | 45 |\\r\\n| Wrong-answer notes under 20 words (verdict only) | 0.0 per question | 1.1 per question |\\r\\n| Wrong-answer notes that run an explicit test (negate it, does it weaken, does it trigger the rule, why should we believe it) | 1.4 of 4 | 0.2 of 4 |\\r\\n| Words in the correct-answer note | 143 | 60 |\\r\\n| Examples, analogies or worked numbers | 2.0 | 0.5 |\\r\\n| Stated generalizations (\\"whenever\\", \\"on X questions\\", \\"usually\\") | 1.6 | 0.3 |\\r\\n\\r\\nWhat actually separates them, in order of how reliably it predicted my judgment:\\r\\n\\r\\n1. **The Evaluate step argues with the argument.** Strong ones do real work before the choices: they build the objection (\\"Given that X, how could we argue not-Y?\\"), do the arithmetic (25% of $400 vs 13% of $1,000), or set the two populations side by side. Loose ones restate the stimulus and move on.\\r\\n2. **The Goal names the answer, not the task.** Strong: \\"look for an answer saying the fish in the leafless trees are exposed to predators that hunt by sight.\\" Loose: \\"Find the flaw that mistakes a response to danger for a cause\\", \\"Identify an answer choice that supports fairness.\\" Strong Goals often give two acceptable shapes (a linking answer or a defender).\\r\\n3. **Every wrong answer gets a reason tied to this question, and usually a test.** The loose group\'s signature is the verdict-only note: \\"This is irrelevant.\\", \\"That is unnecessary.\\", \\"The argument never discusses profits.\\" Strong notes say what the answer claims, what was needed, and where they diverge, often by running the family\'s test on it (negate it; would it weaken; is the trigger established; does the conclusion match the rule\'s right side).\\r\\n4. **The popular trap gets the most attention.** Strong explanations spend their longest wrong-answer note on the choice that drew 15-30% of students and say why it tempts. Loose ones treat it like every other wrong answer.\\r\\n5. **The correct-answer note explains the fit, and often generalizes it.** Strong: \\"Another correct answer performing the same function could have said...\\" (PT146-S2-Q13); the karaoke/Jenga reason why A → C still works when you predicted A and B → C (PT139-S4-Q21). Loose: \\"This is the flaw.\\"\\r\\n6. **Fidelity.** Two loose items are long. Length did not save them: a misquoted answer choice, imported outside facts, or the author disowning the question destroys model value regardless of word count.\\r\\n\\r\\nWhat did not separate them: word count by itself (PT152-S4-Q19 is 479 words and strong; PT158-S2-Q16 is 716 and loose), the choice-tag vocabulary, and the presence of jokes (several strong ones joke; it only hurts when the joke is at the question\'s or the answer\'s expense).\\r\\n\\r\\n## 4. Rubric used for the shortlist\\r\\n\\r\\nGate (hard requirement): full Argument breakdown and all five choice notes. Every shortlisted candidate passed.\\r\\n\\r\\nScored 0 / 1 / 2:\\r\\n\\r\\n| Criterion | 2 | 1 | 0 |\\r\\n|---|---|---|---|\\r\\n| **P** Prediction | Goal names the content or shape of the right answer before the choices (may give two acceptable shapes) | Goal names the task or admits it cannot predict | No prediction |\\r\\n| **X** Precision | Names the exact gap, flaw or relationship in this argument\'s words | Uses the right label but stays generic | Label only, or wrong |\\r\\n| **E** Eliminations | Every wrong answer gets a question-grounded reason; the popular trap gets the most attention | Most do; one or two verdict-only notes | Verdict-only notes |\\r\\n| **T** Transfer | States the move in a form that works on the next question of this type (the test, the rule, the recurring trap) | Transfer is implicit | None |\\r\\n| **C** Clarity | Plain, no padding, no pasted repetition | Some padding or pasted notes | Hard to follow |\\r\\n| **L** Learner value | Teaches what a student needs to understand and repeat the move: why the trap tempts, what to do when stuck, a worked example or analogy, a second correct answer that would also work | Justifies the key well but does not teach the move | Only justifies the key |\\r\\n\\r\\nPlus a **fidelity flag** (not scored; disqualifying if serious): `slip` factual or typo error, `tone` the author mocks the question or answer, `register` explicit-Standard PT157/158 prose, `transcription` damaged text.\\r\\n\\r\\nLearner value is the criterion that most often separated a 10 from a 12. Many explanations justify the key correctly (1) but only the best ones also show the student the move they will reuse (2).\\r\\n\\r\\n## 5. Step structure per family (DB, most common sequences)\\r\\n\\r\\nLabels normalized (parenthetical indicator words dropped: \\"Conclusion (therefore)\\" counts as Conclusion).\\r\\n\\r\\n- **Flaw (524):** Conclusion > Evidence > Evaluate > Goal (67%); Evidence > Conclusion > Evaluate (9%); with an Intermediate Conclusion step (5%). Evaluate usually names the famous flaw or builds the objection; Goal often gives both the formal and the \\"fails to consider\\" phrasing.\\r\\n- **Must Be True / Most Supported (462):** Statements > Evaluate > Goal (45%); Statements only (16%). Statements is often a diagram. Evaluate scans for conditional, causal, math and pivot wording and names the inference to make.\\r\\n- **Necessary Assumption (352):** Conclusion > Evidence > Evaluate > Goal (66%). Goal typically offers a linking answer and a defender answer, and says \\"negate it\\".\\r\\n- **Weaken (335) / Strengthen (277):** Conclusion > Evidence > Evaluate > Goal (59% / 55%). Evaluate uses \\"Given that ... how could we argue that ...\\" or Curious Fact / Alternate Explanation / Plausibility.\\r\\n- **Paradox (213):** \\"Apparent Paradox > Evaluate\\" (22%, older tests) or \\"Given that ... > How can it be that / Why is it that ... > Evaluate > Goal\\" (about 30% combined, often with Evaluate / Goal merged).\\r\\n- **Sufficient Assumption (193):** Conclusion > Evidence > Evaluate > Goal (53%); Evidence > Conclusion > Evaluate (21%). Goal usually diagrams the missing link as the logic path.\\r\\n- **Principle-Strengthen (181):** Conclusion > Evidence > Evaluate > Goal (48%). Goal is \\"If evidence, then conclusion\\" in general terms.\\r\\n- **Principle-Conform (99):** mixed. Conclusion > Evidence > Evaluate > Goal (24%) when the principle is in the answers; Principle > Evaluate > Goal (9%) or Principle > Goal (7%) when it is in the stimulus.\\r\\n- **Main Conclusion (144) / Role (126):** Conclusion > Evidence > Evaluate > Goal (42% / 53%), with an Intermediate Conclusion step in about 8%. Evaluate almost always opens with the reminder that the conclusion usually comes early (first sentence or after a pivot).\\r\\n- **Parallel (100) / Parallel Flaw (123):** Conclusion > Evidence > Evaluate > Goal (49% / 67%), Evidence first in about 19-35%. Goal is an algebraic recipe (P1, P2, C) or a plain-language template.\\r\\n- **Agree/Disagree (119):** speaker-named steps. \\"Speaker\'s Claims > Speaker\'s Responses > Goal\\", often a numbered claim list with \\"Does [speaker 2] disagree?\\" per claim.\\r\\n- **Method (91):** Conclusion > Evidence > Evaluate > Goal (37%) for one speaker; \\"X\'s Conclusion > X\'s Evidence > Y\'s Response > Evaluate > Goal\\" for two speakers.\\r\\n- **Evaluate (28):** Conclusion > Evidence > Evaluate > Goal (75%).\\r\\n\\r\\n## 6. Recurring phrasing conventions\\r\\n\\r\\n- **Frames:** \\"Given that ... how could we (still) argue that ...\\" (Weaken, Strengthen, NA, Flaw); \\"Curious Fact ... Author\'s Explanation ... Alternate Explanation / Plausibility\\" (any causal argument); \\"Given that ... How can it be that ...\\" (Paradox); \\"If Evidence, then Conclusion\\" (Principle, SA).\\r\\n- **Tests in choice notes:** negate it and ask if it weakens (NA); \\"fails to consider X\\" means ask whether X weakens, \\"presumes X\\" means ask whether X is necessary (Flaw); \\"argues X by Y: X must match the conclusion and Y the evidence\\" (Method, Flaw); a rule can only prove its right-hand side (Principle, SA); \\"can we support the Agree position and the Disagree position?\\" (Agree/Disagree); \\"Why should we believe this?\\" (Role, Main Conclusion).\\r\\n- **Recurring trap names:** Too Strong (the most common NA and MBT tag), Out of Scope, No Impact, Unclear Impact, No Distinction (Paradox), Deepens Paradox, Wrong Flaw / Not an Objection (Flaw), Bad Conclusion / Evidence / Trigger Match (Principle, Parallel), Premise / Last-Claim Trap / Opposing Position (Main Conclusion), Unsupported Agree/Disagree Position (Agree/Disagree), Fake Opposite / Illegal Negation (NA), Reversal (SA). Tags come from the local markdown; the dump does not include them.\\r\\n- **Rules stated as rules:** \\"Most X can only be concluded from a Most X premise\\"; \\"some / sometimes / can is almost always wrong on Strengthen, Weaken, Paradox, SA\\"; \\"the second speaker almost never denies the first speaker\'s evidence\\"; \\"about 85-90% of Main Conclusion questions put the conclusion in the first sentence or after a pivot\\"; \\"on EXCEPT, three of the working answers usually work one way and one works another\\".\\r\\n- **Voice habits that should not be copied:** mocking the question or answer (\\"hot garbage\\", \\"so, so dumb\\", \\"unthinkably weird\\", \\"dumpster fire\\"), and the PT157/158 em-dash register.\\r\\n\\r\\n## 7. `Explanations/targeted-questions/`\\r\\n\\r\\nThree files: a README and two explanations, PT134-S1-Q16 (Flaw, belief vs implication, answer A) and PT158-S4-Q24 (Weaken, food-supply chain, answer D). The README says they were \\"added for LSAT U use when Joshua needs specific questions available outside a full PrepTest-section explanation file.\\"\\r\\n\\r\\nThey are not LSAT Lab transcriptions. The layout is different and the parser will not read it: an H1 title, a metadata block (type, answer, LSAT U order, bank file), H2 sections for Stimulus, Prompt, Argument breakdown, **Written explanation** (a free prose section the LSAT Lab files do not have), Answer choice explanations with an italic tag, and a closing **Student takeaway**. The prose is written in LSAT U\'s own voice: no em-dashes, short sentences, jokes built on the stimulus (\\"the drug sneaks into the food supply wearing a tiny trench coat\\", \\"Still a raccoon in the kitchen\\"), and a one-line takeaway (\\"Break the link that matters. That\'s the move.\\").\\r\\n\\r\\nAuthorship: git attributes them to `vnjkoxpl-42905` (Joshua\'s account) inside a bulk commit dated 2026-09-05 titled \\"Update RRC launch page copy\\", with no co-author line and no other reference in the repo or wiki. The style, the house-voice compliance and the README wording point to an AI assistant (Claude or Codex) drafting them for Joshua. That is an inference; the git history cannot confirm who typed them.\\r\\n\\r\\nThey are useful as a picture of the target voice (especially \\"Written explanation\\" and \\"Student takeaway\\", which have no LSAT Lab equivalent). They are not in the golden set because they are outside the corpus and use a different structure.\\r\\n\\r\\n## 8. Caveats\\r\\n\\r\\n- All scores are one reader\'s judgment, formed by reading each candidate once. Treat 11 vs 12 as a tie.\\r\\n- Features and difficulty come from the question bank. 110 explained questions have no bank match, and some shortlisted items have no features in the bank at all (PT143-S1-Q6, PT154-S1-Q10), so the coverage grid undercounts them.\\r\\n- I read about 125 explanations in full or near-full, weighted toward the highest-scoring candidates in each family and difficulty band. I did not read all 3,000, so a better exemplar for a thin cell may exist.\\r\\n---\\r\\ntype: teaching-notes\\r\\nsubject: LR explanation model library, 10 to 20 top-tier models per question type\\r\\nupdated: 2026-09-27\\r\\n---\\r\\n\\r\\n# LR explanation model library\\r\\n\\r\\n334 e…1268 tokens truncated…6-09-27, replaces the \\"18 wrong keys\\" item). [{'type': 'input_text', 'text': 'Script completed\nWall time 5.9 seconds\nOutput:\n'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': '{"chunk_id":"14d22f","wall_time_seconds":0.6159938,"exit_code":0,"original_token_count":2547,"output":"---\\r\\ntype: handoff\\r\\nsubject: LR explanation coverage, full pickup for Codex (or any fresh agent)\\r\\ncreated: 2026-09-27\\r\\nfrom: Claude (Opus 5.5) session d175f546\\r\\n---\\r\\n\\r\\n# Handoff: write the missing LR explanations for LSAT U\\r\\n\\r\\nRead this whole file before doing anything. It is written so a fresh agent can pick up with no other context.\\r\\n\\r\\n## 1. The goal\\r\\n\\r\\nLSAT U (lsatprep.study) shows Logical Reasoning questions from official PrepTests. Each question should have a written explanation in the database. About 560 slots have no explanation or an incomplete one. The job is to write them in the voice and quality of the best existing explanations, load them into the database, and verify them.\\r\\n\\r\\nJoshua Fisseha owns the product and the teaching voice. The explanations are the product: they teach the reasoning, not just the answer.\\r\\n\\r\\n## 2. Joshua\'s method, and where we are in it\\r\\n\\r\\n1. **Golden set** (done): the 51 best existing explanations. `corpus/lr-explanations/golden-set.md`.\\r\\n2. **Model library** (done): 334 top-tier existing explanations, 10 to 20 per question type, with a \\"What the best ones do\\" section per type. `corpus/lr-explanations/models/index.md` + 9 type files.\\r\\n3. **Pilot of 20** (written, NOT loaded, WAITING for Joshua\'s read): `corpus/lr-explanations/drafts/pilot-2026-09-27.md`.\\r\\n4. **The rest in batches** (NOT started). Joshua sees only a preview of each batch and the low-confidence drafts.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Gate: do not write or load step 4 until Joshua has read the pilot and said it is good (or said what to change).** If he has not given a verdict, your first job is to ask him for one.\\r\\n\\r\\n## 3. Rules Joshua set (do not re-ask these)\\r\\n\\r\\n- No intermediate guides, cheat sheets or style docs. Write directly from the models. \\"Don\'t make this complicated.\\"\\r\\n- Use his two PDFs for the named moves and traps: text at `corpus/lr-explanations/work/pilot-inputs/cram.txt` and `guideline.txt` (originals: `C:\\\\Users\\\\Joshu\\\\OneDrive\\\\Desktop\\\\2024-Updated-LR-cram-sheet.pdf`, `C:\\\\Users\\\\Joshu\\\\Downloads\\\\LR GUIDELINE QUESTION TYPE AND REASONING TYPE.pdf`).\\r\\n- For each new explanation, find the closest existing ones by question type, features (Sampling, EXCEPT, Principles in Answers ...) and kind of stimulus, and follow them. The pilot names its models in a \\"Modelled on\\" line.\\r\\n- Voice: plain, direct, short sentences, teach the reasoning. **No em dashes anywhere.** No mocking the question, no political examples, no Logic Games references (there are no Logic Games on the LSAT). The \\"Do not copy from the corpus\\" section of `golden-set.md` lists what not to inherit from old explanations.\\r\\n- Trap tags use the vocabulary the database already uses for that question type.\\r\\n- Dropped by Joshua, do not revive: a writer-mark column; fixing the ensure/insure and Packaging/Packing spellings; per-type writing guides.\\r\\n- Never delete files or records without his explicit yes. Absolute dates only.\\r\\n\\r\\n## 4. Where everything is\\r\\n\\r\\nVault root: `C:\\\\Users\\\\Joshu\\\\dev\\\\ai-os`. Read its `AGENTS.md` (Codex) or `CLAUDE.md` (Claude) first.\\r\\n\\r\\n| What | Path |\\r\\n|---|---|\\r\\n| Decision log for this work (all rounds of Q&A) | `brainstorms/2026-09-26-lr-explanation-coverage.md` |\\r\\n| Golden set | `corpus/lr-explanations/golden-set.md` |\\r\\n| Model library | `corpus/lr-explanations/models/` (start at `index.md`) |\\r\\n| Pilot | `corpus/lr-explanations/drafts/pilot-2026-09-27.md` |\\r\\n| Open-items list (update it as you go) | `corpus/lr-explanations/fixes/index.md` |\\r\\n| Defects found in existing explanations (revamp track, not this job) | `corpus/lr-explanations/fixes/model-library-defects.md`, `revamp-list.md` |\\r\\n| Gap list + helper scripts used for the pilot | `corpus/lr-explanations/work/pilot-inputs/` (`gaps.py` builds `gaps.json`; `neighbors.py`; `show.py`) |\\r\\n| Scoring / neighbor / print scripts | `system/scripts/lr-explanations/score_corpus.py`, `neighbors.py`, `show.py` |\\r\\n| Read-only database dump | `system/scripts/lsatlab-pull/data/db/*.json` (refresh with `python system/scripts/lsatlab-pull/db_pull.py` from the vault root; add `lr_answer_choices` and the four default tables as args to pull all five) |\\r\\n| The question bank (what students see and are graded on) | `projects/lsatu-wt-glossary/public/data/PT*-S*-LR.json` (worktree of the app repo; `projects/lsatu/` is the main checkout) |\\r\\n| SQL load pattern that worked (221 loads, 2026-09-27) | `corpus/lr-explanations/work/load-pattern/` (batch-01 = steps only; batch-01/02/09 have choice notes; batch-23/26/33-36 insert a whole new question) |\\r\\n| Codex\'s independent review of this work | `corpus/lr-explanations/work/codex-review/` (BRIEF.md = what it was asked; round1.txt = its verdict, once saved) |\\r\\n| Session log | `wiki/log.md` (entries 2026-09-26, 2026-09-27) |\\r\\n| Earlier bank repairs (PRs #146 to #154), for background only | `corpus/lr-explanations/fixes/index.md`, \\"Data and question bank\\" |\\r\\n\\r\\n## 5. The data model (verified against the dump 2026-09-27)\\r\\n\\r\\n- `lr_questions` (preptest, section, question_number, stimulus, prompt, correct_choice, question_type, section_id, qid, is_explained, source_note)\\r\\n- `lr_explanations` (question_id, style). Use style `single`. One explanation per question.\\r\\n- `lr_explanation_steps` (explanation_id, position from 0, label, body). The Argument Breakdown. Labels follow the type: e.g. Conclusion / Evidence / Evaluate / Goal; Paradox uses Given That / How Can It Be That / Evaluate-Goal.\\r\\n- `lr_explanation_choice_notes` (explanation_id, letter, body). One per choice A to E.\\r\\n- `lr_answer_choices` (question_id, letter, choice_text, tag, is_correct, student_percent). The trap tag lives here, per choice.\\r\\n\\r\\n**The bank and the database hold each question twice.** The bank shows and grades; the database explains. After any load, compare stimulus and correct answer per slot between the two. The bank key is what students are graded against.\\r\\n\\r\\nGap states in `gaps.json`: Unexplained (question in DB, no explanation), Unloaded (question not in DB at all: insert question + choices + explanation, see batch-23), Notes-only (write the breakdown), Steps-only (write the notes). Counts as of the last build: 348 / 179 / 17 / 16. **`gaps.json` may predate the 221-question load. Re-pull the dump and re-run `gaps.py` before trusting it.**\\r\\n\\r\\n## 6. How to load (after Joshua approves)\\r\\n\\r\\n- The database is reached through the **Lovable connector**, `query_database`, project `53b9a6f2-aba7-4ae4-8dd1-fa5b8aab4d76`. Write SQL in the load-pattern shape: dollar-quoted text (`$lsat$...$lsat$`), look up the explanation by preptest/section/question_number, `ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING`, guards so nothing overwrites an existing step or note.\\r\\n- `query_database` and `deploy_project` are free. **Never use the Lovable agent chat (`send_message`); it spends credits** and needs Joshua\'s explicit yes each time.\\r\\n- Explanations are read from the database at runtime, so a load is live with no deploy. Bank (JSON) changes go through a branch + PR on `vnjkoxpl-42905/lsatu-684d89db` (never commit to main), then free `deploy_project` once `list_edits` shows the commit `completed`.\\r\\n- Before each batch, snapshot any rows you will touch into `raw/sources/lsatu-lr-snapshots/`.\\r\\n\\r\\n## 7. Verify every batch\\r\\n\\r\\nFor each slot: steps exist and positions run 0..n; exactly 5 choice notes; the note for the correct letter matches the **bank** key; every quoted choice matches the bank\'s choice text; zero em dashes (`\\\\u2014`); no emphasis marker glued to a word; stimulus and key agree between bank and DB. Re-pull the dump and re-run `gaps.py`; the gap count should drop by exactly the batch size.\\r\\n\\r\\n## 8. State of the live site (as of 2026-09-27 12:30)\\r\\n\\r\\n- PR #153 (stray PT128-S2-Q26 deleted) and #154 (PT148-S3-Q26 choice D \\"novel\\") are merged and published. #154 verified live by fetching `lsatprep.study/data/PT148-S3-LR.json`.\\r\\n- PRs #138, #141, #142 on the app repo are still open (not part of this job; don\'t merge without Joshua).\\r\\n\\r\\n## 9. Open questions to raise with Joshua first\\r\\n\\r\\n1. His verdict on the 20-question pilot. This gates everything in step 4.\\r\\n2. About 30 answer choices differ in meaning between the bank and the database (list in `fixes/index.md`). Should they be settled against LSAT Lab\'s `/api/questions` (needs LSAT Lab logged in; method in `skills/codex/lsat-lab-sync/SKILL.md`, which is in the catalog but not activated for Codex)? Unanswered.\\r\\n3. Whatever Codex\'s review (section 4) recommends that changes the plan. Put those to him as decisions, don\'t act on them silently.\\r\\n\\r\\n## 9b. Codex review (2026-09-27) and what was done about it\\r\\n\\r\\nFull text: `corpus/lr-explanations/work/codex-review/round1.txt` (VERDICT: REVISE). Accepted and DONE 2026-09-27 by Claude (pilot has a \\"Fixed 2026-09-27 after Codex review\\" note; golden-set picks #2 and #35 are marked REFERENCE ONLY):\\r\\n- Pilot errors: PT120-S4-Q9 (note wrongly rules out A), PT114-S4-Q12 (proof swaps \\"cannot help\\" for \\"cannot affect\\"), PT128-S3-Q16 (invented premise and a \\"10%\\" figure).\\r\\n- Golden picks PT145-S2-Q23 (reversed predicates in the correct-answer note) and PT138-S2-Q25 (calls the \\"only if\\" term sufficient) marked reference-only.\\r\\n- Gap count is at least 567, not 560: also count the 7 slots with a breakdown but only 3 or 4 notes (`fixes/side-issues.md` section 4).\\r\\n- Every load must touch only approved slots and never delete. Do not use the app\'s `scripts/knowledge/ingest-lr-explanations.mjs`; it deletes and rebuilds.\\r\\nRejected: \\"score_corpus.py has a syntax error\\" (it compiles). The scorer only builds shortlists; it is not a quality measure, and nothing publishes on its score.\\r\\n\\r\\n## 10. Next actions, in order\\r\\n\\r\\n1. Read this file, then the decision log, `golden-set.md`, `models/index.md`, the pilot, and the review in `work/codex-review/`.\\r\\n2. Ask Joshua the open questions in section 9.\\r\\n3. When the pilot is approved: revise it as he says, load its 20, verify (section 7).\\r\\n4. Write the rest by type in batches of about 40. Each batch goes into a draft file in `corpus/lr-explanations/drafts/`, with a preview for Joshua and any low-confidence drafts marked. Load only after he approves the batch. Verify, then update `fixes/index.md` and append `wiki/log.md`.\\r\\n"}'}]

It summarizes the Claude-oriented project guidance from `CLAUDE.md` in a smaller format that is fast for Codex to scan. `CLAUDE.md` remains the fuller source of project knowledge.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Fast Locator\\r\\n\\r\\n- Vault root (the `~/ai-os` directory IS the Obsidian vault): `/Users/joshuaf/ai-os`\\r\\n- Code repo root (this project): `/Users/joshuaf/ai-os/projects/lsatu`\\r\\n- Live vault knowledge: `/Users/joshuaf/ai-os/wiki/` (hubs, voice, business), `/Users/joshuaf/ai-os/students/`, `/Users/joshuaf/ai-os/corpus/`\\r\\n- Pre-2026-07-16 LSATU product knowledge (archived, read-only): `/Users/joshuaf/ai-os/archive/wiki-pre-rebuild-2026-07-16/domains/lsat-u/`\\r\\n\\r\\nIf an older note says `~/Documents/Obsidian Vault/...` or `~/Documents/Claude/02_PROJECTS/LSATU`, treat it as stale — the vault was consolidated into `~/ai-os`. Use the absolute paths above.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Project Map\\r\\n\\r\\n- `src/App.tsx`: route registration and top-level app wiring.\\r\\n- `src/pages/`: route-level screens, including the Foyer and admin/teacher surfaces.\\r\\n- `src/components/`: shared UI, including `foyer/`, `inbox/`, and shadcn/ui primitives.\\r\\n- `src/hooks/`: auth, permissions, and shared data hooks.\\r\\n- `src/lib/`: drill, WAJ, gamification, and other product logic.\\r\\n- `src/integrations/supabase/`: generated Supabase client and types. Do not hand-edit generated types.\\r\\n- `supabase/functions/`: Edge Functions.\\r\\n- `supabase/migrations/`: database schema migrations.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Stack And Commands\\r\\n\\r\\n- Stack: React 18, Vite 5, TypeScript, Tailwind, shadcn/ui, Supabase, TanStack Query.\\r\\n- Package manager: `npm`. `package-lock.json` is canonical. Ignore stray Bun lockfiles.\\r\\n- Dev server: `npm run dev` on port `8080`.\\r\\n- Build: `npm run build`\\r\\n- Lint: `npm run lint`\\r\\n- Test: `npm run test`\\r\\n- Watch tests: `npm run test:watch`\\r\\n- Typecheck: `npm run typecheck`. Required before a push — `vite build` strips types without checking them, so a type error would ship. `.claude/safe-push.sh` gates on it.\\r\\n- E2E: `npm run test:e2e` (Playwright, `e2e/smoke.spec.ts`).\\r\\n\\r\\n## Working Rules\\r\\n\\r\\n- Keep changes small and aligned with existing patterns.\\r\\n- Use TypeScript React function components and the `@/` path alias.\\r\\n- Preserve Lovable-specific plumbing, including the `lovable-tagger` Vite plugin, the PWA setup, and the service-worker guard in `src/main.tsx`.\\r\\n- Do not change app behavior just to satisfy tooling setup.\\r\\n- Do not hardcode secrets or service-role credentials.\\r\\n- Treat Supabase-generated files as generated artifacts, not hand-edited source.\\r\\n- TypeScript strictness is intentionally loose. Do not tighten it without explicit approval.\\r\\n- Keep this adapter aligned with `CLAUDE.md` without duplicating the full project guide. Put durable detail in `CLAUDE.md`; keep shared reference material in `docs/`, `references/`, or `templates/`.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Product Constraints\\r\\n\\r\\n- The Foyer is the primary navigation surface. The `GooeyDock` quick-launch row belongs only on `/foyer`.\\r\\n- Inbox is a real 1:1 student-admin messaging system with PDF attachments.\\r\\n- Admin surfaces must stay responsive on desktop and mobile.\\r\\n- Prefer truthful fallbacks over fake buttons, fake analytics, or placeholder behavior.\\r\\n- Onboarding asks for name, not username. Sign-in uses email. The instructor display name is `Joshua`.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Smart Drill Note\\r\\n\\r\\nOn `/drill`, adaptive Smart Drill can show a `currentQuestion` that is not the same as `session.questionQueue[session.currentIndex]`. Any per-question UI state must key off `currentQuestion.qid` unless the code path is explicitly limited to queued drills.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Verification (must not skip)\\r\\n\\r\\n- Any change to `src/components/` or `src/pages/` is not \\"done\\" until it has been viewed running in the app (dev server + a browser tool), with a screenshot or DOM check as evidence. Never report a UI change complete on the strength of the diff alone. This is the single biggest recurring failure across past sessions — a hard gate, not a suggestion.\\r\\n- **Responsive fixes carry a wider gate: verify across the full emulate matrix BEFORE pushing, not after Joshua reports it.** Narrow, mid, and wide viewports plus the sidecar-docked case, every time. A pushed \\"fix\\" that does not hold costs a second full session; this has already happened once.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Workflow Expectations\\r\\n\\r\\n- Run `npm run build` before wrapping up significant code or documentation work.\\r\\n- If tests are relevant to the change, run them too.\\r\\n- After completing a change, stage the intended files, commit with a concise message, and push to `origin main` unless the user says otherwise.\\r\\n- **Push via `.claude/safe-push.sh`, not raw `git push`.** Lovable auto-commits to `main` mid-session, so a raw push can be rejected or race a concurrent commit. The script fetches, merges `origin/main` if it diverged (never rebases, never force-pushes), then pushes. It also gates on `npm run typecheck`.\\r\\n- **Never roll `main` back.** Do not use Lovable\'s \\"Revert to commit\\", \\"revert and resend\\", or history restore, and never reset or force-push `main`. On 2026-09-27 an accidental \\"revert and resend\\" (b998596) silently undid three merged PRs across 121 question-bank files, and students saw the wrong questions again. To undo your own work, `git revert <your SHA>` only. If a Lovable dialog offers a revert, dismiss it; if one fires, stop and tell Joshua before repairing anything.\\r\\n- Existing Codex repo hooks live in `.codex/hooks.json`; preserve them unless the task explicitly requires a hook change.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Session Protocol (second brain)\\r\\n\\r\\nProduct context, status, and decisions live in the Obsidian vault at `~/ai-os/`, not this repo. Two repo-local skills in `.agents/skills/` run the session ritual; the canonical protocol is in the vault `CLAUDE.md`.\\r\\n\\r\\nThe vault was rebuilt 2026-07-16 (paths relative to `~/ai-os`). Live: teaching voice `wiki/teaching-voice.md` (route ALL student-facing copy through it), the business `wiki/entities/aspiring-attorneys.md`, a student\'s record `students/<name>/` (see `students/README.md`), teaching knowledge `corpus/index.md`, and the `wiki/{tasks,hot,dashboard,index,log}.md` hubs named below. Archived read-only reference (pre-rebuild product knowledge, may be stale): `archive/wiki-pre-rebuild-2026-07-16/domains/lsat-u/` — product context `…/synthesis/intro-to-lr-product.md`, design system `…/synthesis/intro-to-lr-design.md`, quality bar `…/synthesis/bootcamp-quality-bar.md`, domain index `…/index.md`.\\r\\n\\r\\n- `pickup` — start of session. Read `wiki/tasks.md` (the Now / Next / Done ledger) first, then `wiki/hot.md`, `wiki/dashboard.md`, skim `wiki/index.md`, read the latest `wiki/log.md` entry. Emit a 6-8 line briefing (where we left off, Now, Next, open threads, hot, suggested next) and wait. Do not deep-read other pages until pointed at a task.\\r\\n- `wrapup` — end of session. Propose which `## Now` tasks finished and confirm before writing; a wrong \\"Done\\" is the failure to avoid. Then strike finished items through in `wiki/tasks.md` with a date, refresh `wiki/hot.md`, append a dated `wiki/log.md` entry (newest first, distinct label for same-day sessions), update the task anchor.\\r\\n\\r\\n`wiki/tasks.md` is the single source of truth for what is open vs done. Finished work is struck through so a new session never re-runs it. `raw/` in the vault is immutable.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Codex Adapter Layout\\r\\n\\r\\n- `.codex/config.toml`: minimal shared Codex project config. Keep secrets and machine-specific overrides out of this file.\\r\\n- `.codex/agents/`: Codex agent definitions converted from `.claude/agents/*.md` when Claude agents exist. Use `.toml` files with converted instructions in `developer_instructions`.\\r\\n- `.agents/skills/`: repository-local Codex skills copied from `.claude/skills/` when Claude skills exist and Codex should use them. Keep each `SKILL.md` with its supporting files.\\r\\n- `.codex/hooks.json`: existing repository hooks. Preserve unless the task explicitly concerns hooks.\\r\\n\\r\\nThis checkout currently has Claude settings and hooks under `.claude/`, but no `.claude/skills/` or `.claude/agents/` directory to convert.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Known Gaps\\r\\n\\r\\n- Vitest specs exist (80 `src/**/*.test.ts(x)` files as of 2026-07-16); run with `npm run test`. Playwright is wired with one e2e spec (`e2e/smoke.spec.ts`), run via `npm run test:e2e`.\\r\\n- No Prettier config and no CI workflows.\\r\\n- `README.md` is still minimal.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Agent skills\\r\\n"}'}]

## assistant

Recovered artifact history supports a broader project than the current rewrite wave.

**Ultimate goal:** LSAT U should give students a complete, trustworthy authored explanation for every official LR question, integrated into their practice and review. Each explanation should teach how to reason through this particular question and give students a move they can repeat on similar questions. Correctness, teaching value, source fidelity, and the delivered app experience all matter. “All questions have rows” was only the coverage milestone.

**Earliest verified app foundation:**

- App Git contains explanation-path activity from **2026-07-11** and a bulk **2026-07-10** tree commit, but generic titles do not establish exact transcript/import scope.
- **2026-08-02 `29bfde76`** added authoritative correct-answer lines to PT141–159 and recovered two questions.
- **2026-08-03 `c52b3665`** (“surface the authored LSAT Lab explanations”) explains the original integration problem: the `lr_*` tables held 3,768 verified questions, but the app’s Explanation button opened the AI tutor. Students never saw authored explanations.
  - Added `useLrExplanation`, `ExplanationPanel`, and an Answer button.
  - Rendered source argument breakdown, answer tags/student percentages, and individual choice explanations.
  - Showed exactly the source’s available styles. Three styles meant a selector; a single style meant none.
  - Where a source alternate said “same as Standard,” borrowed and labeled Standard’s breakdown.
  - AI tutor became fallback only for approximately 350 video-only questions.

**Quality standard:**

- Real Evaluate reasoning before choices, rather than summary.
- Goal predicts concrete answer content/shape, rather than restating the stem.
- Every wrong answer gets a reason grounded in this question, often running its appropriate test.
- Tempting/popular traps deserve added explanation.
- Correct answer teaches the reusable reasoning move.
- Fidelity disqualifies misquotes, invented premises, outside facts, reversed conditional/quantifier logic, and corrupted diagrams even when prose is long.
- Length alone is no quality measure.
- Joshua rejected fixed “shorter sentences” / “one idea per step” prescriptions. Use a strong framework with question-specific fluidity.
- New writing is Standard only (`single` for the database’s single-style cases). Preserve and audit existing `standard`, `brief`, `another_way`, and `single` styles; alternatives are legitimate distinct source content, not duplicates.
- September handoff also prohibits mocking questions, political examples, Logic Games references, and em dashes; corpus model weaknesses must be read before imitation.
- Joshua later dropped writer attribution and intermediate/per-type writing guides. Do not revive superseded complexity.
